American English · October 9, 2026

Scientists’ profiles: fixes applied and second review

Fixes applied

272 of 272 confirmed issues (L001–L272) from the previous report were applied. The edits affect 92 distinct English fields, synchronized across 10 files (368 file-level field edits). The full, issue-by-issue record and exact before/after field values are included below.

Names, factual claims, and date/quantity values were retained. Approved formatting changes include date separators, two American-style written dates, and “1000s” → “thousands.” The 79 earlier verification questions were not resolved or substantively corrected. Where an approved typography fix overlaps one of those passages, the carried-over excerpt reflects that typography.

Step 2 is review only. None of the new suggestions below has been applied. “New” means not listed in the previous report; it includes issues noticed in newly rewritten passages. “Carried over” identifies the 79 previous Needs verification items and preserves their V001–V079 IDs.

Current wording   Suggested wording   Needs verification. Insertions and deletions may have highlighting on only one side.

New findingsCarried-over verificationAll 272 applied fixesExact field changes
Review scope and conventions

All 83 corrected English profile names, rendered credential labels, fields, titles/affiliations, and biographies were reread. The active chunked data, monolithic fallback, and matching deployment copies were checked. The older, unused translation mapping i18n/scientists-profiles-en.json is outside the active-page source and was not edited. Azerbaijani text, identity fields, degree codes, profile order, and HTML markup remain unchanged.

American English is retained: closed compounds where conventional, consistent Ph.D. notation, sentence case for descriptive roles and fields, en dashes for ranges, and American quotation punctuation. Official names, transliterations, degree equivalences, ranks, and technical claims require verification where indicated. This is a language review, not an external fact-check.

Each row counts as one finding, including repeated instances of a convention within a profile. Consistency findings are classified by their underlying Orthography, Grammar, or Style concern. The 114 language findings and 91 verification questions are counted separately. Excerpts are HTML-decoded and whitespace-normalized; exact JSON field values appear in the audit section.

The earlier report remains unchanged. Applied fixes were checked against their original issue IDs. The rollback patch passed git apply --reverse --check reports/scientists-profile-fixes.patch. The backup and change log contain exact pre-fix content and before/after SHA-256 fingerprints.

Although he has been living and creating in Germany for more than 35 years, he remains a citizen of Azerbaijan.</p><p class="bio-section-title">Contributions to nonlinear analysis and mathematical physics</p><p class="bio">He created a new theory of topological invariants for the global solution of nonlinear equations.</p><p class="bio">These invariants play an important role in the analysis of qualitative changes (local and global bifurcation) of nonlinear systems.</p><p class="bio-section-title">Mathematical modeling</p><p class="bio">He conducted fundamental research on mathematical modeling of problems encountered in the fields of biology, medicine, ecology, mechanics and physics.</p><p class="bio">He created new classes of attractors of dynamic systems according to evolution equations and made an important contribution to their systematic study.</p><p class="bio-section-title">Activities in international scientific journals</p><p class="bio">He is a member of the editorial board of more than ten leading international magazines.</p><p class="bio">He is the editor-in-chief of &quot;International Journal of Biomathematics and Biostatistics&quot;.</p><p class="bio-section-title">Scientific publications</p><p class="bio">He is the author of 8 books published in Japan, Germany, USA, Canada and Switzerland.</p><p class="bio">He has written more than 200 scientific articles and collaborated with co-authors from 15 countries.</p><p class="bio-section-title">Activities in international universities</p><p class="bio">In 2018, he lectured for 6 months at the University of Toronto and the Fields Institute as a &quot;Dean&#x27;s Distinguished Visiting Professor&quot; and played an important role in the implementation of the thematic program &quot;Emerging Challenges in Mathematical Biology&quot;.</p><p class="bio">In 2019, he was invited to the University of Waterloo with the status of &quot;James D. 44. Akif Alaferdov — bio_html_en (L150, L151, L152, L153)
i18n/scientists-profiles.json:754 · /profiles/43/bio_html_en
i18n/scientists-profiles/chunk-03.json:1 · /profiles/1/bio_html_en
Deployment/i18n/scientists-profiles.json:754 · /profiles/43/bio_html_en

Exact original field value

<p class="bio bio-lead">Akif Alaferdov is a well-known traumatologist, a descendant of Azerbaijanis who were deported to Kyrgyzstan in 1933.</p><p class="bio">Dr. Akif made important contributions to the methodology of surgical treatment of femoral neck fractures. McMurray received a patent for the invention of the &quot;detachable fixator&quot; used in surgery. Akif Alaferdov is the author of a monograph, more than 35 scientific articles, 3 methodical materials and more than 20 effective recommendations on the treatment of femoral neck fractures with surgical intervention.</p><p class="bio">During his professional career, Alaferdov was the head of the complex trauma department at the National Hospital of the Ministry of Health of the Kyrgyz Republic and a member of the Special Scientific Council of the Kyrgyz State Medical Institute. He has performed thousands of operations and trained highly qualified specialists. His services in this field were evaluated by the President of the Kyrgyz Republic with an Honorary Order in 1997, and in 2003 he was awarded the title of &quot;Honored Doctor&quot;.</p><p class="bio">In addition to his achievements in the field of medicine, Alaferdov was also engaged in social and cultural activities. During the years 1997-2013, he was the chairman of the &quot;Azeri&quot; Public Union of Azerbaijanis living in Kyrgyzstan. Under his leadership, Sunday schools in the Azerbaijani language were opened, mosques were built, and a folklore ensemble called &quot;Stars&quot; was created. He took part in many events to strengthen the relations between Azerbaijan and Kyrgyzstan, organized &quot;Bishkek-Baku&quot; telebridges for many years with the participation of deputies, writers, public figures and diaspora representatives representing both sides.</p><p class="bio">For his long-term public activity, Akif Alaferdov was awarded the &quot;Dank&quot; state award in 2008, as well as the medal &quot;For service in Diaspora activity&quot; from the State Committee for Work with the Diaspora of the Republic of Azerbaijan. In 2023, he was awarded the Order of &quot;Friend&quot; by the President of Kyrgyzstan for his contributions to strengthening peace, friendship and cooperation between nations. He was awarded the &quot;Chingiz Aytmatov&quot; medal for his achievements in the fields of culture, science, literature and social activities.</p><p class="bio">He was awarded the &quot;Progress&quot; medal in 2026 by order of the President of the Republic of Azerbaijan Mr. Ilham Aliyev for his services in the field of strengthening friendship between peoples and the development of the Azerbaijani diaspora.</p><div class="card-resources"><a class="btn btn-primary" href="akif-alaferdov-media.html">Additional resources <svg viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" aria-hidden="true"><path d="M5 12h14M13 6l6 6-6 6" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"></path></svg></a></div>

<p class="bio bio-lead">He is a graduate of the Faculty of Mechanics and Mathematics of the Moscow State University named after M. V. Lomonosov and his postgraduate studies.</p><p class="bio">He is a well-known Soviet, Russian and Azerbaijani scientist in the field of development of mathematical models and methods for the study of wave and oscillation processes in the creation of piezoelectric devices and apparatus used in the world ocean and outer space research, marine seismic exploration, electronics, defense and medical industries.</p><p class="bio">For the first time, he managed to solve the problem of determining the optimal shape of piezoelectric elements, which has not been solved for many years, meets the physical properties required in the dynamic mode, and has a minimum mass or volume. The solution to this problem made it possible to determine the location and individual characteristics of objects moving under water in the world ocean, the information of interest to experts in relevant scientific fields was recorded from space objects - artificial satellites and spaceships, and the creation of receiver-transmitter devices to transmit and receive the Flight Control Center. For the first time, he successfully applied the method of solving two- and three-dimensional dynamic problems, which are important in the creation of piezoelectric devices used in military equipment. The existence of the &quot;edge effect&quot; in disk-shaped piezoceramic membranes was also theoretically discovered by him for the first time. The piezoceramic actuator, developed jointly with the co-authors, was successfully used to solve the problem of creating an artificial heart. He is the author of more than 450 open and closed (confidential) scientific works, inventions, monographs, higher education textbooks, a number of scientific articles on the problems of organizing higher professional education and educational technologies. Under his scientific guidance, 7 graduate students, including 3 Azerbaijanis, defended their candidate dissertations. In general, based on the obtained scientific results, he delivered more than 60, including 12 commissioned plenary scientific reports, at 30 international and general congresses, congresses, conferences and symposia.</p><p class="bio">Some of the prizes and awards received by Professor Aladdin Allahverdiyev for his achievements in the fields of science, technology and education:</p><p class="bio">Medal named after the Hero of Socialist Labor of the Russian Cosmonautics Federation, lieutenant general, Lenin Prize laureate, professor, chairman of the State Commission on Space Programs Georgy Alexandrovich Tyulin;</p><p class="bio">Medal named after Khalil Ahmadovich Rakhmatulin, Hero of Socialist Labor of the National Committee for Theoretical and Applied Mechanics of the Russian Academy of Sciences, academician, laureate of four Orders of Lenin, several State Prizes;</p><p class="bio">the title &quot;Honored worker of higher vocational education of the Russian Federation&quot;;</p><p class="bio">Three times - in 1975, 1977 and 1980 - badges of &quot;The winner of the race of socialism&quot; established by the Central Committee of the Communist Party of the Soviet Union, the former Council of Ministers of the USSR, the Central Committee of Trade Unions, the Central Committee of the All-Union Labor Party;</p><p class="bio">&quot;850th anniversary of Moscow&quot; state medal;</p><p class="bio">Jubilee medal &quot;250th anniversary of MSU named after M.V.
